<content>
1: # Introduction to the codebase of wren-ai-service
2: 
3: ## Table of Contents
4: 
5: - [Purpose](#purpose)
6: - [Environment Setup and Start wren-ai-service Locally](#environment-setup-and-start-wren-ai-service-locally)
7: - [Codebase Introduction](#codebase-introduction)
8:     - [Entrypoint](#entrypoint)
9:     - [Globals](#globals)
10:     - [API endpoints](#api-endpoints)
11:     - [Services](#services)
12:     - [Pipelines](#pipelines)
13:     - [Providers](#providers)
14:     - [Others](#others)
15: 
16: ## Purpose
17: 
18: This document aims to dive deep to the implementation details of wren-ai-service. We have two goals in mind while writing the document:
19: 1. You will be more knowledgeable about how wren-ai-service works under the hood.
20: 2. You will be more confident on what part of codebase is needed for adjustment if you would like to be Wren AI's contributor.
21: 
22: ## Environment Setup and Start wren-ai-service Locally
23: 
24: If you haven't setup the environment or don't know how to run wren-ai-service locally, please refer to the [document](../README.md#setup-for-local-development) here first.
25: 
26: ## Codebase Introduction
27: 
28: wren-ai-service is basically an AI service which provides REST api endpoints for access. There are 4 main concepts to wren-ai-service: `API endpoints`, `Services`, `Pipelines` and `Providers`.
29: 1. `API endpoints`: They are entry points for users to access several kinds of RAG(retrieval-augmented-generation) systems; you can also see API endpoints as encapsulation of Services. For example, when users need to ask a question in order to get SQL, they need to call `/ask` and there is AskService under the hood for background computation.
30: 2. `Services`: They are abstraction of business-logic concepts, such as AskService for users asking questions to get SQL results back, AskDetailsService for users to get SQL breakdown as several sub-steps in order to understand the logic behind the original SQL. Every service is composed of a series of pipelines.
31: 3. `Pipelines`: Basically RAG systems are actually implemented here. However, not all pipelines have complete indexing, retrieval and generation components; it depends on what's the purpose of the pipeline. Also, every pipeline contains some providers such as LLM provider, which represents an LLM.
32: 4. `Providers`: Now there are 4 kinds of providers:
33:     - llm: representing large language models, and now we support OpenAI, Azure OpenAI, OpenAI api-compatible and Ollama models
34:     - embedder: representing embedding models, and now we support OpenAI, Azure OpenAI, OpenAI api-compatible and Ollama models
35:     - document store: representing vector database, and now we use Qdrant
36:     - engine: representing data engine, which is responsible for validating generated SQL's syntax.
37: 
38: ### Entrypoint
39: 
40: - The entry point of wren-ai-service is located at [`wren-ai-service/src/__main__.py`](../src/__main__.py)
41: - The main point of the entry point is the `lifespan` method, which is FastAPI's feature for defining startup and shutdown logic.
42: 
43: ```python
44: # https://fastapi.tiangolo.com/advanced/events/#lifespan
45: @asynccontextmanager
46: async def lifespan(app: FastAPI):
47:     # startup events
48: 
49:     pipe_components = generate_components()
50:     app.state.service_container = create_service_container(
51:         pipe_components,
52:         column_indexing_batch_size=(
53:             int(os.getenv("COLUMN_INDEXING_BATCH_SIZE"))
54:             if os.getenv("COLUMN_INDEXING_BATCH_SIZE")
55:             else 50
56:         ),
57:         table_retrieval_size=(
58:             int(os.getenv("TABLE_RETRIEVAL_SIZE"))
59:             if os.getenv("TABLE_RETRIEVAL_SIZE")
60:             else 10
61:         ),
62:         table_column_retrieval_size=(
63:             int(os.getenv("TABLE_COLUMN_RETRIEVAL_SIZE"))
64:             if os.getenv("TABLE_COLUMN_RETRIEVAL_SIZE")
65:             else 1000
66:         ),
67:         query_cache={
68:             # the maxsize is a necessary parameter to init cache, but we don't want to expose it to the user
69:             # so we set it to 1_000_000, which is a large number
70:             "maxsize": 1_000_000,
71:             "ttl": int(os.getenv("QUERY_CACHE_TTL") or 120),
72:         },
73:     )
74:     app.state.service_metadata = create_service_metadata(pipe_components)
75:     init_langfuse()
76: 
77:     yield
78: 
79:     # shutdown events
80:     langfuse_context.flush()
81: ```
82: 
83: - For startup logic, we initialize pipeline components, service containers(which include all services), service metadata(which is some metadata logged for traces inside [Langfuse, an open-source LLM engineering platform](https://langfuse.com/)) and Langfuse.
84: - For initializing pipeline components, we are in the progress of supporting multiple LLMs, namely users can choose which LLM is responsible for each pipeline.
85:     - You still need to have `.env.dev` locally, then you can prepare `config.yaml` and run `just start`.
86: - For shutdown logic, we make sure all Langfuse events are transmitted successfully
87: 
88: ### Globals
89: 
90: - The file is located at [`wren-ai-service/src/globals.py`](../src/globals.py)
91: - You can understand the details of service containers and service metadata here
92:     - service containers(Other services are not supported in UI yet)
93:         - SemanticsPreparationService: this is responsible for indexing [MDL](https://docs.getwren.ai/oss/engine/concept/what_is_mdl) to Qdarnt
94:         - AskService: this is responsible for answering users' questions with SQLs, namely text-to-sql
95:         - AskDetailsService: this is responsible for SQL breakdown to several sub-steps
96:     - service metadata
97:         - We will record llm's and embedding model's metadata, wren-ai-service version, etc.
98: 
99: ### API endpoints
100: 
101: - All business related API endpoints are located at [`wren-ai-service/src/web/v1/routers`](../src/web/v1/routers)
102: - Since computation for each kind of API endpoint(ex. ask, etc.) takes several seconds, so we use FastAPI's `background_tasks`. For example, after the `ask` api is invoked, the response is immediately returned, then users need to conduct polling in order to get the latest task status; and once the status is `finished`, the result is returned correspondingly
103: - Each kind of API endpoint corresponds to one kind of business related task, for example, AskService, AskDetailsService
104: 
105: ### Services
106: 
107: - All services are located at [`wren-ai-service/src/web/v1/services`](../src/web/v1/services)
108: 
109: ### Pipelines
110: 
111: - All pipelines are located at [`wren-ai-service/src/pipelines`](../src/pipelines)
112: - Since all pipelines are actually RAG systems, so we classify the role of each pipeline as indexing, retrieval or generation
113: - The abstract class is defined at [`wren-ai-service/src/core/pipeline.py`](../src/core/pipeline.py)
114: 
115: ### Providers
116: 
117: - All providers are located at [`wren-ai-service/src/providers`](../src/providers)
118: - The abstract classes for providers(LLM, embedding model and document store) are defined at [`wren-ai-service/src/core/provider.py`](../src/core/provider.py)
119: - The abstract class for engine is defined at [`wren-ai-service/src/core/engine.py`](../src/core/engine.py)

(End of file - total 119 lines)
</content>
```

<content>
1: # AI Service Configuration
2: 
3: The AI service configuration is managed through a combination of environment variables and a configuration file, providing a flexible and secure approach to setting up the service.
4: 
5: 1. **Environment Variables**:
6: 
7:    - Used for configuring sensitive information such as vendor API keys
8:    - Specify the configuration file to use
9:    - Allow for partial settings to be configured directly, see [Settings Loading Mechanism](#settings-loading-mechanism) for more details
10:    - Provide a way to override settings in different environments
11: 
12: 2. **Configuration File**:
13:    - Used for detailed configuration of components, pipelines, and other service settings
14:    - Allows for more complex and structured configuration options
15: 
16: This dual approach ensures that sensitive data can be kept secure (using environment variables) while allowing for more detailed and shareable configuration through the configuration file. It also provides flexibility in deployment across different environments.
17: 
18: ## Settings Loading Mechanism
19: 
20: The AI service uses a hierarchical approach to load settings, ensuring flexibility across different environments and deployment scenarios. The settings are loaded in the following order of precedence:
21: 
22: 1. **Default Values**: These are defined as class attributes in the `Settings` class within [`config.py`](../src/config.py). They serve as the base configuration.
23: 
24: 2. **Environment Variables**: Using [pydantic-settings](https://fastapi.tiangolo.com/advanced/settings/#pydantic-settings), the service checks for environment variables that match the setting names. If found, these override the default values. For example, `WREN_AI_SERVICE_HOST` can override the default `host` value.
25: 
26: 3. **.env.dev File**: The service loads additional settings or overrides existing ones from a `.env.dev` file if present. This is particularly useful for development environments.
27: 
28: 4. **config.yaml File**: This file provides the highest priority configuration. It can override all previous settings and is used to configure components, pipelines, and other detailed settings. See [Configuration File](#configuration-file) for more details.
29: 
30: This mechanism allows for easy configuration management across different environments, from development to production, while maintaining security for sensitive information like API keys.
31: 
32: ## Configuration File
33: 
34: The configuration file (`config.yaml`) is structured into several sections, each defining different aspects of the AI service. Here's a breakdown of its main components:
35: 
36: 1. **LLM Configuration**:
37: 
38:    ```yaml
39:    type: llm
40:    provider: <provider_name>
41:    models:
42:      - model: <model_name>
43:        kwargs: {}
44:    api_base: <api_endpoint>
45:    ```
46: 
47:    This component initializes the LLM provider at runtime. You can specify multiple models with different parameters. The `kwargs` field allows for model-specific configurations. For example:
48: 
49:    ```yaml
50:    type: llm
51:    provider: openai_llm
52:    models:
53:      - model: gpt-4
54:        kwargs:
55:          temperature: 0
56:          n: 1
57:          max_tokens: 4096
58:          response_format:
59:            type: "json_object"
60:      - model: gpt-4o-mini
61:        kwargs: {}
62:    api_base: https://api.openai.com/v1
63:    ```
64: 
65:    For detailed parameter options, refer to the implementation of the specific LLM provider.
66: 
67: 2. **Embedder Configuration**:
68: 
69:    ```yaml
70:    type: embedder
71:    provider: <provider_name>
72:    models:
73:      - model: <model_name>
74:        dimension: <embedding_size>
75:    api_base: <api_endpoint>
76:    timeout: <timeout_in_seconds>
77:    ```
78: 
79:    This component configures the embedder, which converts text into numerical vectors. The `provider` specifies the embedder service (e.g., OpenAI, Ollama). You can define multiple `models` with their parameters. The `dimension` parameter indicates the size of the embedding vector.
80: 
81: 3. **Engine Configuration**:
82: 
83:    ```yaml
84:    type: engine
85:    provider: <provider_name>
86:    endpoint: <engine_endpoint>
87:    ```
88: 
89:    This component configures the engine responsible for generating SQL queries. The `provider` specifies the engine service (e.g., Wren UI).
90: 
91: 4. **Document Store Configuration**:
92: 
93:    ```yaml
94:    type: document_store
95:    provider: <provider_name>
96:    ```
97: 
98:    This component configures the document store, which is responsible for storing and retrieving embeddings. The `provider` specifies the document store service (e.g., Qdrant).
99: 
100: 5. **Pipeline Configuration**:
101: 
102:    ```yaml
103:    type: pipeline
104:    pipes:
105:      - name: <pipe_name>
106:        llm: <provider>.<model_name>
107:        embedder: <provider>.<model_name>
108:        engine: <provider_name>
109:        document_store: <provider_name>
110:    ```
111: 
112:    This component configures each pipeline, specifying different LLM, embedder, engine, and document store combinations. For LLM and embedder, use `<provider>.<model_name>`. For engine and document store, use `<provider_name>`.
113: 
114:    Example:
115: 
116:    ```yaml
117:    type: pipeline
118:    pipes:
119:      - name: sql_generation
120:        llm: openai_llm.gpt-4o-mini
121:        engine: wren_ui
122:    ```
123: 
124: 6. **Settings**:
125: 
126:    ```yaml
127:    settings:
128:      host: <host_address>
129:      port: <port_number>
130:      column_indexing_batch_size: <batch_size>
131:      table_retrieval_size: <retrieval_size>
132:      table_column_retrieval_size: <column_retrieval_size>
133:      query_cache_maxsize: <cache_size>
134:      query_cache_ttl: <cache_ttl_in_seconds>
135:      langfuse_host: <langfuse_endpoint>
136:      langfuse_enable: <true/false>
137:      logging_level: <log_level>
138:      development: <true/false>
139:    ```
140: 
141:    This section defines various service settings including host, port, indexing and retrieval parameters, cache settings, Langfuse configuration, logging level, and development mode.
142: 
143: This configuration file allows for detailed customization of the AI service components, pipelines, and overall behavior. It provides a centralized place to manage complex configurations while keeping sensitive information separate (managed through environment variables). See [Full Configuration File](../tools/config/config.full.yaml) for a complete example.

(End of file - total 143 lines)
</content>
```

2: import pathlib
3: from dataclasses import dataclass
4: 
5: 
6: @dataclass
7: class DBConfig:
8:     ROOT_DIR: pathlib.Path = pathlib.Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent.parent
9:     DEFAULT_SQLITE_PATH: pathlib.Path = ROOT_DIR / "data" / "Chinook.db"
10:     CONFIG_DIR: pathlib.Path = ROOT_DIR / ".app_config"
11:     CONFIG_FILE: pathlib.Path = CONFIG_DIR / "config.json"
12:     ACTIVE_DATABASE_URI_KEY: str = "database_uri"
13: 
14:     def default_database_uri(self) -> str:
15:         return self.sqlite_path_to_uri(self.DEFAULT_SQLITE_PATH)
16: 
17:     def sqlite_path_to_uri(self, db_path: pathlib.Path | str) -> str:
18:         path = pathlib.Path(db_path).expanduser().resolve()
19:         return f"sqlite:///{path}"
20: 
21:     def _ensure_config_dir(self) -> None:
22:         self.CONFIG_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
23: 
24:     def save_config(self, data: dict) -> None:
25:         self._ensure_config_dir()
26:         self.CONFIG_FILE.write_text(json.dumps(data, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
27: 
28:     def load_config(self) -> dict:
29:         if not self.CONFIG_FILE.exists():
30:             return {}
31:         try:
32:             raw = self.CONFIG_FILE.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
33:             data = json.loads(raw)
34:             if isinstance(data, dict):
35:                 return data
36:         except Exception:
37:             return {}
38:         return {}
39: 
40:     def get_database_uri(self) -> str:
41:         config = self.load_config()
42:         uri = config.get(self.ACTIVE_DATABASE_URI_KEY)
43:         if isinstance(uri, str) and uri.strip():
44:             return uri.strip()
45:         return self.default_database_uri()
46: 
47:     def set_database_uri(self, uri: str) -> None:
48:         clean_uri = uri.strip()
49:         if not clean_uri:
50:             raise ValueError("Database URI cannot be empty.")
51:         config = self.load_config()
52:         config[self.ACTIVE_DATABASE_URI_KEY] = clean_uri
53:         self.save_config(config)

34: ## Why Wren Engine
35: 
36: AI agents can already call tools, browse docs, and write code. What they still struggle with is business context.
37: 
38: Enterprise data is not just rows in a warehouse. It is definitions, metrics, relationships, permissions, lineage, and intent. An agent that can connect to PostgreSQL or Snowflake still does not know what "net revenue", "active customer", or "pipeline coverage" actually mean in your company.
39: 
40: This is not just our thesis. In [Your Data Agents Need Context](https://a16z.com/your-data-agents-need-context/), a16z argues that data agents break down when they only have connectivity and SQL generation, but lack business definitions, source-of-truth context, and the operational knowledge that explains how a company actually runs.
41: 
42: <p align="center">
43:   <img width="888" height="678" alt="before Wren Engine Challenge" src="https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/62e2cbe6-ee28-4417-ba69-805de35d3187" />
44: </p>
45: 
46: Wren Engine exists to solve that gap.
47: 
48: It gives AI agents a context engine they can reason over, so they can:
49: 
50: - understand models instead of raw tables
51: - use trusted metrics instead of inventing SQL
52: - follow relationships instead of guessing joins
53: - respect governance instead of bypassing it
54: - turn natural language into accurate, explainable data access
55: 
56: This is the open source context engine for teams building the next generation of agent experiences.
57: 
58: ## The Vision
59: 
60: We believe the future of AI is not tool calling alone. It is context-rich systems where agents can reason, retrieve, plan, and act on top of a shared understanding of business reality.
61: 
62: Wren Engine is our open source contribution to that future.
63: 
64: <p align="center">
65:   <img width="1267" height="705" alt="with_wren_engine" src="https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/3a6531fe-4731-4f21-ae9a-786b219f3c0e" />
66: </p>
67: 
68: It is the semantic and execution foundation beneath Wren AI, and it is designed to be useful well beyond a single product:
69: 
70: - embedded in MCP servers and agent workflows
71: - connected to modern warehouses, databases, and file systems
72: - expressive enough to model business meaning through MDL
73: - robust enough to support governed enterprise use cases
74: - open enough for the community to extend, integrate, and build on
75: 
76: If Wren AI is the full vision, Wren Engine is the open core that makes that vision interoperable.
77: 
78: ## What Wren Engine Does
79: 
80: Wren Engine turns business data into agent-usable context.
81: 
82: <p align="center">
83:   <img width="2199" height="1537" alt="engine-architecture" src="https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/302351e7-9ac3-4916-99f6-972d33aee076" />
84: </p>
85: 
86: At a high level:
87: 
88: 1. You describe your business domain with Wren's semantic model and MDL.
89: 2. Wren Engine captures the context agents need: models, metrics, relationships, and access rules.
90: 3. It analyzes intent and plans correct queries across your underlying data sources.
91: 4. MCP clients and AI agents interact with that context through a clean interface.
92: 5. Teams keep refining the model as business logic and systems evolve.
93: 
94: This is the practical open source path from text-to-SQL toward context-aware data agents.
95: 
96: That means your agent is no longer asking, "Which raw table should I query?"
97: 
98: It is asking, "Which business concept, metric, or governed slice of context do I need to answer this task correctly?"
99: 
100: ## Built For Agent Builders
101: 
102: Wren Engine is especially useful for the open source community building agent-native workflows in tools like:
103: 
104: - OpenClaw
105: - Claude Code
106: - VS Code
107: - Claude Desktop
108: - Cline
109: - Cursor
110: 
111: If your environment can speak MCP, call HTTP APIs, or embed a semantic service, Wren Engine can become the context layer behind your agent.
112: 
113: Use it to power experiences like:
114: 
115: - natural-language analytics with trusted business definitions
116: - AI copilots that can answer questions across governed enterprise data
117: - agents that generate dashboards, reports, and workflow decisions
118: - code assistants that need real business context, not just schema dumps
119: - internal AI tools that should be grounded in semantic models instead of ad hoc SQL
120: 
121: This is especially important in developer-facing agent environments, where the assistant may understand your codebase but still lacks the business context required to answer data questions correctly.
122: 
123: ## Supported Data Sources
124: 
125: Wren Engine is built to work across modern data stacks, including warehouses, databases, and file-based sources.
126: 
127: Current open source support includes connectors such as:
128: 
129: - Amazon S3
130: - Apache Spark
131: - Apache Doris
132: - Athena
133: - BigQuery
134: - ClickHouse
135: - Databricks
136: - DuckDB
137: - Google Cloud Storage
138: - Local files
139: - MinIO
140: - MySQL
141: - Oracle
142: - PostgreSQL
143: - Redshift
144: - SQL Server
145: - Snowflake
146: - Trino
147: 
148: See the connector API docs in the project documentation for the latest connection schemas and capabilities.
149: 
150: ## Get Started
151: 
152: ### Use Wren Engine through AI Agents
153: 
154: If you want to use Wren Engine from a Claude Code or other AI Agents, start here:
155: 
156: - [Installation](https://docs.getwren.ai/oss/engine/get_started/installation)
157: - [Quick start: Chat with jaffle_shop using Wren Engine + Claude Code](https://docs.getwren.ai/oss/engine/get_started/quickstart)
158: - [Understanding Wren AI project structure](https://docs.getwren.ai/oss/engine/guide/modeling/wren_project)
159: 
160: The MCP server includes:
161: 
162: - a local Web UI for connection and MDL setup
163: - read-only mode for safer agent usage
164: - manifest deployment and validation tools
165: - metadata tools for remote schema discovery
166: 
167: ### Learn the concepts
168: 
169: - [What is context?](https://docs.getwren.ai/oss/engine/concept/what_is_context)
170: - [What is Modeling Definition Language (MDL)?](https://docs.getwren.ai/oss/engine/concept/what_is_mdl)
171: - [Benefits of Wren Engine with LLMs](https://docs.getwren.ai/oss/engine/concept/benefits_llm)
172: - [Your Data Agents Need Context](https://a16z.com/your-data-agents-need-context/)
173: - [Powering Semantic SQL for AI Agents with Apache DataFusion](https://getwren.ai/post/powering-semantic-sql-for-ai-agents-with-apache-datafusion)
174: 
175: 
176: 
177: ## Wren Engine vs. Other Data Tools
178: 
179: People often compare Wren Engine to catalog services like DataHub, raw database MCP servers, BI semantic tools, or text-to-SQL agents.
180: 
181: The simple difference is:
182: 
183: - those tools usually help an agent find data or generate SQL
184: - Wren Engine helps an agent understand business meaning and produce the right query through a context engine
185: 
186: | Tool type | What it gives the agent | What Wren Engine adds |
187: | --- | --- | --- |
188: | Data catalog services | Tables, columns, lineage, owners, descriptions | Business models, metrics, relationships, and governed query planning |
189: | Raw database or schema access | Direct access to schemas and SQL execution | A business layer above raw tables so the agent does not have to guess intent |
190: | BI or semantic tools | Curated metrics and entities for analytics workflows | An open context layer designed for MCP and agent workflows |
191: | Text-to-SQL agents | Fast SQL generation from natural language | Better accuracy by grounding generation in explicit business definitions |
192: 
193: Many teams will want both:
194: 
195: - a catalog to inventory and document the data estate
196: - Wren Engine to turn that data into agent-ready context
197: 
198: Why that matters:
199: 
200: - more accurate answers because joins and metrics are defined instead of guessed
201: - more consistent answers because every agent uses the same business definitions
202: - safer data access because governance can be carried into query planning
203: - less prompt engineering because the context lives in the engine, not in the prompt
204: 
205: Without Wren, an agent may know where the data is but still not know how to answer the question correctly.
206: 
207: ## Repository Map
208: 
209: This repository contains the core engine modules:
210: 
211: | Module | What it does |
212: | --- | --- |
213: | [`wren-core`](./wren-core) | Rust context engine powered by Apache DataFusion for MDL analysis, planning, and optimization |
214: | [`wren-core-base`](./wren-core-base) | Shared manifest and modeling types |
215: | [`wren-core-py`](./wren-core-py) | PyO3 bindings that expose the engine to Python |
216: | [`ibis-server`](./ibis-server/) | FastAPI server for query execution, validation, metadata, and connectors |
217: | [`mcp-server`](./mcp-server/) | MCP server for AI agents and MCP-compatible clients |
218: 
219: Supporting modules include `wren-core-legacy`, `example`, `mock-web-server`, and benchmarking utilities.
220: 
221: ### Developer entry points
222: 
223: - [`wren-core/README.md`](./wren-core/README.md)
224: - [`wren-core-py/README.md`](./wren-core-py/README.md)
225: - [`ibis-server/README.md`](./ibis-server/README.md)
226: - [`mcp-server/README.md`](./mcp-server/README.md)
227: 
228: ## Local Development
229: 
230: Common workflows:
231: 
232: ```bash
233: # Rust context engine
234: cd wren-core
235: cargo check --all-targets
236: 
237: # Python + connector server
238: cd ibis-server
239: just install
240: just dev
241: 
242: # MCP server
243: cd mcp-server
244: # see module README for uv-based setup
245: ```
246: 
247: ## Project Status
248: 
249: Wren Engine is actively evolving in the open. The current focus is to make the context engine, execution path, and MCP integration stronger for real-world agent workflows.
250: 
251: If you are building with agents today, this is a great time to get involved.
252: 
253: ## Community
254: 
255: - Join our [Discord community](https://discord.gg/5DvshJqG8Z)
256: - Open a [GitHub issue](https://github.com/Canner/wren-engine/issues)
257: - Explore [Wren AI](https://github.com/Canner/WrenAI) to see the broader product vision
258: - Read the market thesis from a16z: [Your Data Agents Need Context](https://a16z.com/your-data-agents-need-context/)
259: 
260: Wren Engine is for builders who believe AI needs better context, not just better prompts.

(End of file - total 260 lines)
</content>

31: class SemanticsPreparationStatusRequest(BaseModel):

46: class SemanticsPreparationService:

48:         self,
49:         pipelines: Dict[str, BasicPipeline],
50:         maxsize: int = 1_000_000,

52:     ):
53:         self._pipelines = pipelines
54:         self._prepare_semantics_statuses: Dict[
55:             str, SemanticsPreparationStatusResponse

58:     @observe(name="Prepare Semantics")

67:                 "error_type": "",

72: 
73:         try:

133:                 ),
134:             )
135:

1: import asyncio
2: import logging
3: from typing import Dict, List, Literal, Optional
4: 
5: from cachetools import TTLCache

7: from pydantic import AliasChoices, BaseModel, Field
8: 
9: from src.core.pipeline import BasicPipeline
10: from src.utils import trace_metadata
11: from src.web.v1.services import BaseRequest, SSEEvent
12: 
13: logger = logging.getLogger("wren-ai-service")
14: 
15: 
16: class AskHistory(BaseModel):
17:     sql: str
18:     question: str
19: 
20: 
21: # POST /v1/asks
22: class AskRequest(BaseRequest):
23:     query: str
24:     # don't recommend to use id as a field name, but it's used in the older version of API spec
25:     # so we need to support as a choice, and will remove it in the future
26:     mdl_hash: Optional[str] = Field(validation_alias=AliasChoices("mdl_hash", "id"))
27:     histories: Optional[list[AskHistory]] = Field(default_factory=list)
28:     ignore_sql_generation_reasoning: bool = False
29:     enable_column_pruning: bool = False
30:     use_dry_plan: bool = False
31:     allow_dry_plan_fallback: bool = True
32:     custom_instruction: Optional[str] = None
33: 
34: 
35: class AskResponse(BaseModel):
36:     query_id: str
37: 
38: 
39: # PATCH /v1/asks/{query_id}
40: class StopAskRequest(BaseRequest):
41:     status: Literal["stopped"]
42: 
43: 
44: class StopAskResponse(BaseModel):
45:     query_id: str
46: 
47: 
48: # GET /v1/asks/{query_id}/result
49: class AskResult(BaseModel):
50:     sql: str
51:     type: Literal["llm", "view"] = "llm"
52:     viewId: Optional[str] = None
53: 
54: 
55: class AskError(BaseModel):
56:     code: Literal["NO_RELEVANT_DATA", "NO_RELEVANT_SQL", "OTHERS"]
57:     message: str
58: 
59: 
60: class AskResultRequest(BaseModel):
61:     query_id: str
62: 
63: 
64: class _AskResultResponse(BaseModel):
65:     status: Literal[
66:         "understanding",
67:         "searching",
68:         "planning",
69:         "generating",
70:         "correcting",
71:         "finished",
72:         "failed",
73:         "stopped",
74:     ]
75:     rephrased_question: Optional[str] = None
76:     intent_reasoning: Optional[str] = None
77:     sql_generation_reasoning: Optional[str] = None
78:     type: Optional[Literal["GENERAL", "TEXT_TO_SQL"]] = None
79:     retrieved_tables: Optional[List[str]] = None
80:     response: Optional[List[AskResult]] = None
81:     invalid_sql: Optional[str] = None
82:     error: Optional[AskError] = None
83:     trace_id: Optional[str] = None
84:     is_followup: bool = False
85:     general_type: Optional[
86:         Literal["MISLEADING_QUERY", "DATA_ASSISTANCE", "USER_GUIDE"]
87:     ] = None
88: 
89: 
90: class AskResultResponse(_AskResultResponse):
91:     is_followup: Optional[bool] = Field(False, exclude=True)
92:     general_type: Optional[
93:         Literal["MISLEADING_QUERY", "DATA_ASSISTANCE", "USER_GUIDE"]
94:     ] = Field(None, exclude=True)
95: 
96: 
97: class AskService:
98:     def __init__(
99:         self,
100:         pipelines: Dict[str, BasicPipeline],
101:         allow_intent_classification: bool = True,
102:         allow_sql_generation_reasoning: bool = True,
103:         allow_sql_functions_retrieval: bool = True,
104:         allow_sql_diagnosis: bool = True,
105:         allow_sql_knowledge_retrieval: bool = True,
106:         enable_column_pruning: bool = False,
107:         max_sql_correction_retries: int = 3,
108:         max_histories: int = 5,
109:         maxsize: int = 1_000_000,
110:         ttl: int = 120,
111:     ):
112:         self._pipelines = pipelines
113:         self._ask_results: Dict[str, AskResultResponse] = TTLCache(
114:             maxsize=maxsize, ttl=ttl
115:         )
116:         self._allow_sql_generation_reasoning = allow_sql_generation_reasoning
117:         self._allow_sql_functions_retrieval = allow_sql_functions_retrieval
118:         self._allow_intent_classification = allow_intent_classification
119:         self._allow_sql_diagnosis = allow_sql_diagnosis
120:         self._allow_sql_knowledge_retrieval = allow_sql_knowledge_retrieval
121:         self._enable_column_pruning = enable_column_pruning
122:         self._max_histories = max_histories
123:         self._max_sql_correction_retries = max_sql_correction_retries
124: 
125:     def _is_stopped(self, query_id: str, container: dict):
126:         if (
127:             result := container.get(query_id)
128:         ) is not None and result.status == "stopped":
129:             return True
130: 
131:         return False
132: 
133:     @observe(name="Ask Question")
134:     @trace_metadata
135:     async def ask(
136:         self,
137:         ask_request: AskRequest,
138:         **kwargs,
139:     ):
140:         trace_id = kwargs.get("trace_id")
141:         results = {
142:             "ask_result": {},
143:             "metadata": {
144:                 "type": "",
145:                 "error_type": "",
146:                 "error_message": "",
147:                 "request_from": ask_request.request_from,
148:             },
149:         }
150: 
151:         query_id = ask_request.query_id
152:         histories = ask_request.histories[: self._max_histories][
153:             ::-1
154:         ]  # reverse the order of histories
155:         rephrased_question = None
156:         intent_reasoning = None
157:         sql_generation_reasoning = None
158:         sql_samples = []
159:         instructions = []
160:         api_results = []
161:         table_names = []
162:         error_message = None
163:         invalid_sql = None
164:         allow_sql_generation_reasoning = (
165:             self._allow_sql_generation_reasoning
166:             and not ask_request.ignore_sql_generation_reasoning
167:         )
168:         enable_column_pruning = (
169:             self._enable_column_pruning or ask_request.enable_column_pruning
170:         )
171:         allow_sql_functions_retrieval = self._allow_sql_functions_retrieval
172:         allow_sql_diagnosis = self._allow_sql_diagnosis
173:         allow_sql_knowledge_retrieval = self._allow_sql_knowledge_retrieval
174:         max_sql_correction_retries = self._max_sql_correction_retries
175:         current_sql_correction_retries = 0
176:         use_dry_plan = ask_request.use_dry_plan
177:         allow_dry_plan_fallback = ask_request.allow_dry_plan_fallback
178:         sql_knowledge = None
179: 
180:         try:
181:             user_query = ask_request.query
182: 
183:             # ask status can be understanding, searching, generating, finished, failed, stopped
184:             # we will need to handle business logic for each status
185:             if not self._is_stopped(query_id, self._ask_results):
186:                 self._ask_results[query_id] = AskResultResponse(
187:                     status="understanding",
188:                     trace_id=trace_id,
189:                     is_followup=True if histories else False,
190:                 )
191: 
192:                 historical_question = await self._pipelines["historical_question"].run(
193:                     query=user_query,
194:                     project_id=ask_request.project_id,
195:                 )
196: 
197:                 # we only return top 1 result
198:                 historical_question_result = historical_question.get(
199:                     "formatted_output", {}
200:                 ).get("documents", [])[:1]
201: 
202:                 if historical_question_result:
203:                     api_results = [
204:                         AskResult(
205:                             **{
206:                                 "sql": result.get("statement"),
207:                                 "type": "view" if result.get("viewId") else "llm",
208:                                 "viewId": result.get("viewId"),
209:                             }
210:                         )
211:                         for result in historical_question_result
212:                     ]
213:                     sql_generation_reasoning = ""
214:                 else:
215:                     # Run both pipeline operations concurrently
216:                     sql_samples_task, instructions_task = await asyncio.gather(
217:                         self._pipelines["sql_pairs_retrieval"].run(
218:                             query=user_query,

66:     async def _model_preprocessor(

69:         def _column_preprocessor(

84:         async def _preprocessor(model: Dict[str, Any], **kwargs) -> Dict[str, Any]:

101:             }

141:             table_name = model["name"]
142:             payload = {
143:                 "type": "TABLE",
144:                 "comment": comment,
145:                 "name": table_name,
146:             }
147:             return {"name": table_name, "payload": str(payload)}
148: 
149:         def _column_command(column: Dict[str, Any], model: Dict[str, Any]) -> dict:
150:             if column.get("relationship"):
151:                 return None
152: 
153:             comments = [
154:                 helper(column, model=model)
155:                 for helper in helper.COLUMN_COMMENT_HELPERS.values()
156:                 if helper.condition(column)
157:             ]
158: 
159:             return {
160:                 "type": "COLUMN",
161:                 "comment": "".join(comments),
162:                 "name": column["name"],
163:                 "data_type": column["type"],
164:                 "is_primary_key": column["name"] == model["primaryKey"],
165:             }
166: 
167:         def _relationship_command(
168:             relationship: Dict[str, Any],
169:             table_name: str,
170:             primary_keys_map: Dict[str, str],
171:         ) -> dict:
172:             condition = relationship.get("condition", "")
173:             join_type = relationship.get("joinType", "")
174:             models = relationship.get("models", [])
175: 
176:             if len(models) != 2:
177:                 return None
178: 
179:             if table_name not in models:
180:                 return None
181: 
182:             if join_type not in ["MANY_TO_ONE", "ONE_TO_MANY", "ONE_TO_ONE"]:
183:                 return None
184: 
185:             # Get related table and foreign key column
186:             is_source = table_name == models[0]
187:             related_table = models[1] if is_source else models[0]
188:             condition_parts = condition.split(" = ")
189:             fk_column = condition_parts[0 if is_source else 1].split(".")[1]
190: 
191:             # Build foreign key constraint
192:             fk_constraint = f"FOREIGN KEY ({fk_column}) REFERENCES {related_table}({primary_keys_map[related_table]})"
193: 
194:             return {
195:                 "type": "FOREIGN_KEY",
196:                 "comment": f'-- {{"condition": {condition}, "joinType": {join_type}}}\n  ',
197:                 "constraint": fk_constraint,
198:                 "tables": models,
199:             }
200: 
201:         def _column_batch(
202:             model: Dict[str, Any], primary_keys_map: Dict[str, str]
203:         ) -> List[dict]:
204:             commands = [
205:                 _column_command(column, model) for column in model["columns"]
206:             ] + [
207:                 _relationship_command(relationship, model["name"], primary_keys_map)
208:                 for relationship in relationships
209:             ]
210: 
211:             filtered = [command for command in commands if command is not None]
212: 
213:             return [
214:                 {
215:                     "name": model["name"],
216:                     "payload": str(
217:                         {
218:                             "type": "TABLE_COLUMNS",
219:                             "columns": filtered[i : i + column_batch_size],
220:                         }
221:                     ),
222:                 }
223:                 for i in range(0, len(filtered), column_batch_size)
224:             ]
225: 
226:         # A map to store model primary keys for foreign key relationships
227:         primary_keys_map = {model["name"]: model["primaryKey"] for model in models}
228: 
229:         return [
230:             command
231:             for model in models
232:             for command in _column_batch(model, primary_keys_map)
233:             + [_model_command(model)]
234:         ]
235: 
236:     def _convert_views(self, views: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> List[Dict[str, str]]:
237:         def _payload(view: Dict[str, Any]) -> dict:
238:             return {
239:                 "type": "VIEW",
240:                 "comment": f"/* {view['properties']} */\n"
241:                 if "properties" in view
242:                 else "",
243:                 "name": view["name"],
244:                 "statement": view["statement"],
245:             }
246: 
247:         return [
248:             {"name": view["name"], "payload": str(_payload(view))} for view in views
249:         ]
250: 
251:     def _convert_metrics(self, metrics: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> List[Dict[str, str]]:
252:         def _create_column(name: str, data_type: str, comment: str) -> dict:
253:             return {
254:                 "type": "COLUMN",
255:                 "comment": comment,
256:                 "name": name,
257:                 "data_type": data_type,
258:             }
259: 
260:         def _dimensions(metric: Dict[str, Any]) -> List[dict]:
261:             return [
262:                 _create_column(
263:                     name=dim.get("name", ""),
264:                     data_type=dim.get("type", ""),
265:                     comment="-- This column is a dimension\n  ",
266:                 )
267:                 for dim in metric.get("dimension", [])
268:             ]
269: 
270:         def _measures(metric: Dict[str, Any]) -> List[dict]:
271:             return [
272:                 _create_column(
273:                     name=measure.get("name", ""),
274:                     data_type=measure.get("type", ""),
275:                     comment=f"-- This column is a measure\n  -- expression: {measure['expression']}\n  ",
276:                 )
277:                 for measure in metric.get("measure", [])
278:             ]
279: 
280:         def _payload(metric: Dict[str, Any]) -> dict:
281:             return {
282:                 "type": "METRIC",
283:                 "comment": f"\n/* This table is a metric */\n/* Metric Base Object: {metric['baseObject']} */\n",
284:                 "name": metric["name"],
285:                 "columns": _dimensions(metric) + _measures(metric),
286:             }
287: 
288:         return [
289:             {"name": metric["name"], "payload": str(_payload(metric))}
290:             for metric in metrics
291:         ]
292: 
293: 
294: ## Start of Pipeline
295: @observe(capture_input=False, capture_output=False)
296: @extract_fields(dict(mdl=Dict[str, Any]))
297: def validate_mdl(mdl_str: str, validator: MDLValidator) -> Dict[str, Any]:
298:     res = validator.run(mdl=mdl_str)
299:     return dict(mdl=res["mdl"])
300: 
301: 
302: @observe(capture_input=False)
303: async def chunk(
304:     mdl: Dict[str, Any],
305:     chunker: DDLChunker,
306:     column_batch_size: int,
307:     project_id: Optional[str] = None,
308: ) -> Dict[str, Any]:
309:     return await chunker.run(
310:         mdl=mdl,
311:         column_batch_size=column_batch_size,
312:         project_id=project_id,
313:     )
314: 
315: 
316: @observe(capture_input=False, capture_output=False)
317: async def embedding(chunk: Dict[str, Any], embedder: Any) -> Dict[str, Any]:
318:     return await embedder.run(documents=chunk["documents"])
319: 
320: 
321: @observe(capture_input=False, capture_output=False)
322: async def clean(
323:     embedding: Dict[str, Any],
324:     cleaner: DocumentCleaner,
325:     project_id: Optional[str] = None,
326: ) -> Dict[str, Any]:
327:     await cleaner.run(project_id=project_id)
328:     return embedding
329: 
330: 
331: @observe(capture_input=False)
332: async def write(clean: Dict[str, Any], writer: DocumentWriter) -> None:
333:     return await writer.run(documents=clean["documents"])
334: 
335: 
336: ## End of Pipeline
337: 
338: 
339: class DBSchema(BasicPipeline):
340:     def __init__(
341:         self,
342:         embedder_provider: EmbedderProvider,
343:         document_store_provider: DocumentStoreProvider,
344:         column_batch_size: int = 50,
345:         **kwargs,
346:     ) -> None:
347:         dbschema_store = document_store_provider.get_store()
348: 
349:         self._components = {
350:             "cleaner": DocumentCleaner([dbschema_store]),
351:             "validator": MDLValidator(),
352:             "embedder": embedder_provider.get_document_embedder(),
353:             "chunker": DDLChunker(),
354:             "writer": AsyncDocumentWriter(
355:                 document_store=dbschema_store,
356:                 policy=DuplicatePolicy.OVERWRITE,
357:             ),
358:         }
359:         self._configs = {
360:             "column_batch_size": column_batch_size,
361:         }
362:         self._final = "write"
363: 
364:         helper.load_helpers()
365:         super().__init__(
366:             AsyncDriver({}, sys.modules[__name__], result_builder=base.DictResult())
367:         )
368: 
369:     @observe(name="DB Schema Indexing")
370:     async def run(
371:         self, mdl_str: str, project_id: Optional[str] = None
372:     ) -> Dict[str, Any]:
373:         logger.info(
374:             f"Project ID: {project_id}, DB Schema Indexing pipeline is running..."
375:         )
376:         return await self._pipe.execute(
377:             [self._final],
378:             inputs={
379:                 "mdl_str": mdl_str,
380:                 "project_id": project_id,
381:                 **self._components,
382:                 **self._configs,
383:             },
384:         )
385: 
386:     @observe(name="Clean Documents for DB Schema")
387:     async def clean(self, project_id: Optional[str] = None) -> None:
388:         await clean(
389:             embedding={"documents": []},
390:             cleaner=self._components["cleaner"],
391:             project_id=project_id,
392:         )

401:                             "table_name": content["name"],
402:                             "table_ddl": _build_view_ddl(content),
403:                         }
404:                     )
405: 
406:         return {
407:             "retrieval_results": retrieval_results,
408:             "has_calculated_field": has_calculated_field,
409:             "has_metric": has_metric,
410:             "has_json_field": has_json_field,
411:         }
412:     else:
413:         retrieval_results = check_using_db_schemas_without_pruning["db_schemas"]
414: 
415:         return {
416:             "retrieval_results": retrieval_results,
417:             "has_calculated_field": check_using_db_schemas_without_pruning[
418:                 "has_calculated_field"
419:             ],
420:             "has_metric": check_using_db_schemas_without_pruning["has_metric"],
421:             "has_json_field": check_using_db_schemas_without_pruning["has_json_field"],
422:         }
423: 
424: 
425: ## End of Pipeline
426: class MatchingTableContents(BaseModel):
427:     chain_of_thought_reasoning: list[str]
428:     columns: list[str]
429: 
430: 
431: class MatchingTable(BaseModel):
432:     table_name: str
433:     table_contents: MatchingTableContents
434:     table_selection_reason: str
435: 
436: 
437: class RetrievalResults(BaseModel):
438:     results: list[MatchingTable]
439: 
440: 
441: RETRIEVAL_MODEL_KWARGS = {
442:     "response_format": {
443:         "type": "json_schema",
444:         "json_schema": {
445:             "name": "retrieval_schema",
446:             "schema": RetrievalResults.model_json_schema(),
447:         },
448:     }
449: }
450: 
451: 
452: class DbSchemaRetrieval(BasicPipeline):
453:     def __init__(
454:         self,
455:         llm_provider: LLMProvider,
456:         embedder_provider: EmbedderProvider,
457:         document_store_provider: DocumentStoreProvider,
458:         table_retrieval_size: int = 10,
459:         table_column_retrieval_size: int = 100,
460:         **kwargs,
461:     ):
462:         self._components = {
463:             "embedder": embedder_provider.get_text_embedder(),
464:             "table_retriever": document_store_provider.get_retriever(
465:                 document_store_provider.get_store(dataset_name="table_descriptions"),
466:                 top_k=table_retrieval_size,
467:             ),
468:             "dbschema_retriever": document_store_provider.get_retriever(
469:                 document_store_provider.get_store(),
470:                 top_k=table_column_retrieval_size,
471:             ),
472:             "table_columns_selection_generator": llm_provider.get_generator(
473:                 system_prompt=table_columns_selection_system_prompt,
474:                 generation_kwargs=RETRIEVAL_MODEL_KWARGS,
475:             ),
476:             "generator_name": llm_provider.get_model(),
477:             "prompt_builder": PromptBuilder(
478:                 template=table_columns_selection_user_prompt_template
479:             ),
480:         }
481: 
482:         # for the first time, we need to load the encodings
483:         _model = llm_provider.get_model()
484:         if "gpt-4o" in _model or "gpt-4o-mini" in _model:
485:             _encoding = tiktoken.get_encoding("o200k_base")
486:         else:
487:             _encoding = tiktoken.get_encoding("cl100k_base")
488: 
489:         self._configs = {
490:             "encoding": _encoding,
491:             "context_window_size": llm_provider.get_context_window_size(),
492:         }
493: 
494:         super().__init__(
495:             AsyncDriver({}, sys.modules[__name__], result_builder=base.DictResult())
496:         )
497: 
498:     @observe(name="Ask Retrieval")
499:     async def run(
500:         self,
501:         query: str = "",
502:         tables: Optional[list[str]] = None,
503:         project_id: Optional[str] = None,
504:         histories: Optional[list[AskHistory]] = None,
505:         enable_column_pruning: bool = False,
506:     ):
507:         logger.info("Ask Retrieval pipeline is running...")
508:         return await self._pipe.execute(
509:             ["construct_retrieval_results"],
510:             inputs={
511:                 "query": query,
512:                 "tables": tables,
513:                 "project_id": project_id or "",
514:                 "histories": histories or [],
515:                 "enable_column_pruning": enable_column_pruning,
516:                 **self._components,
517:                 **self._configs,
518:             },
519:         )

<content>
1: import logging
2: import sys
3: from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional
4: 
5: from hamilton import base
6: from hamilton.async_driver import AsyncDriver
7: from haystack import Document, component
8: from haystack_integrations.document_stores.qdrant import QdrantDocumentStore
9: from langfuse.decorators import observe
10: 
11: from src.core.pipeline import BasicPipeline
12: from src.core.provider import DocumentStoreProvider, EmbedderProvider
13: from src.pipelines.common import ScoreFilter
14: 
15: logger = logging.getLogger("wren-ai-service")
16: 
17: 
18: @component
19: class OutputFormatter:
20:     @component.output_types(
21:         documents=List[Optional[Dict]],
22:     )
23:     def run(self, documents: List[Document]):
24:         list = []
25: 
26:         for doc in documents:
27:             formatted = {
28:                 "question": doc.content,
29:                 "sql": doc.meta.get("sql"),
30:             }
31:             list.append(formatted)
32: 
33:         return {"documents": list}
34: 
35: 
36: ## Start of Pipeline
37: @observe(capture_input=False)
38: async def count_documents(
39:     store: QdrantDocumentStore, project_id: Optional[str] = None
40: ) -> int:
41:     filters = (
42:         {
43:             "operator": "AND",
44:             "conditions": [
45:                 {"field": "project_id", "operator": "==", "value": project_id},
46:             ],
47:         }
48:         if project_id
49:         else None
50:     )
51:     document_count = await store.count_documents(filters=filters)
52:     return document_count
53: 
54: 
55: @observe(capture_input=False, capture_output=False)
56: async def embedding(count_documents: int, query: str, embedder: Any) -> dict:
57:     if count_documents:
58:         return await embedder.run(query)
59: 
60:     return {}
61: 
62: 
63: @observe(capture_input=False)
64: async def retrieval(embedding: dict, project_id: str, retriever: Any) -> dict:
65:     if embedding:
66:         filters = (
67:             {
68:                 "operator": "AND",
69:                 "conditions": [
70:                     {"field": "project_id", "operator": "==", "value": project_id},
71:                 ],
72:             }
73:             if project_id
74:             else None
75:         )
76: 
77:         res = await retriever.run(
78:             query_embedding=embedding.get("embedding"),
79:             filters=filters,
80:         )
81:         return dict(documents=res.get("documents"))
82: 
83:     return {}
84: 
85: 
86: @observe(capture_input=False)
87: def filtered_documents(
88:     retrieval: dict,
89:     score_filter: ScoreFilter,
90:     sql_pairs_similarity_threshold: float,
91:     sql_pairs_retrieval_max_size: int,
92: ) -> dict:
93:     if retrieval:
94:         return score_filter.run(
95:             documents=retrieval.get("documents"),
96:             score=sql_pairs_similarity_threshold,
97:             max_size=sql_pairs_retrieval_max_size,
98:         )
99: 
100:     return {}
101: 
102: 
103: @observe(capture_input=False)
104: def formatted_output(
105:     filtered_documents: dict, output_formatter: OutputFormatter
106: ) -> dict:
107:     if filtered_documents:
108:         return output_formatter.run(documents=filtered_documents.get("documents"))
109: 
110:     return {"documents": []}
111: 
112: 
113: ## End of Pipeline
114: 
115:

60:     ) -> None:

62:             "operator": "AND",

33: def convert_haystack_documents_to_qdrant_points(
34:     documents: List[Document],
35:     *,
36:     use_sparse_embeddings: bool,
37: ) -> List[rest.PointStruct]:
38:     points = []
39:     for document in documents:
40:         payload = document.to_dict(flatten=True)
41:         if use_sparse_embeddings:
42:             vector = {}
43: 
44:             dense_vector = payload.pop("embedding", None)
45:             if dense_vector is not None:
46:                 vector[DENSE_VECTORS_NAME] = dense_vector
47: 
48:             sparse_vector = payload.pop("sparse_embedding", None)
49:             if sparse_vector is not None:
50:                 sparse_vector_instance = rest.SparseVector(**sparse_vector)
51:                 vector[SPARSE_VECTORS_NAME] = sparse_vector_instance
52: 
53:         else:
54:             vector = payload.pop("embedding") or {}
55:         _id = convert_id(payload.get("id"))
56: 
57:         point = rest.PointStruct(
58:             payload=payload,
59:             vector=vector,
60:             id=_id,
61:         )
62:         points.append(point)
63:     return points
64:

66: class AsyncQdrantDocumentStore(QdrantDocumentStore):
67:     def __init__(
68:         self,
69:         location: Optional[str] = None,
70:         url: Optional[str] = None,
71:         port: int = 6333,
72:         grpc_port: int = 6334,
73:         prefer_grpc: bool = False,
74:         https: Optional[bool] = None,
75:         api_key: Optional[Secret] = None,
76:         prefix: Optional[str] = None,
77:         timeout: Optional[int] = None,
78:         host: Optional[str] = None,
79:         path: Optional[str] = None,
80:         force_disable_check_same_thread: bool = False,
81:         index: str = "Document",
82:         embedding_dim: int = 768,
83:         on_disk: bool = False,
84:         use_sparse_embeddings: bool = False,
85:         sparse_idf: bool = False,
86:         similarity: str = "cosine",
87:         return_embedding: bool = False,
88:         progress_bar: bool = True,
89:         recreate_index: bool = False,
90:         shard_number: Optional[int] = None,
91:         replication_factor: Optional[int] = None,
92:         write_consistency_factor: Optional[int] = None,
93:         on_disk_payload: Optional[bool] = None,
94:         hnsw_config: Optional[dict] = None,
95:         optimizers_config: Optional[dict] = None,
96:         wal_config: Optional[dict] = None,
97:         quantization_config: Optional[dict] = None,
98:         init_from: Optional[dict] = None,
99:         wait_result_from_api: bool = True,
100:         metadata: Optional[dict] = None,
101:         write_batch_size: int = 100,
102:         scroll_size: int = 10_000,
103:         payload_fields_to_index: Optional[List[dict]] = None,
104:     ):
105:         super(AsyncQdrantDocumentStore, self).__init__(
106:             location=location,
107:             url=url,
108:             port=port,
109:             grpc_port=grpc_port,
110:             prefer_grpc=prefer_grpc,
111:             https=https,
112:             api_key=api_key,
113:             prefix=prefix,
114:             timeout=timeout,
115:             host=host,
116:             path=path,
117:             force_disable_check_same_thread=force_disable_check_same_thread,
118:             index=index,
119:             embedding_dim=embedding_dim,
120:             on_disk=on_disk,
121:             use_sparse_embeddings=use_sparse_embeddings,
122:             sparse_idf=sparse_idf,
123:             similarity=similarity,
124:             return_embedding=return_embedding,
125:             progress_bar=progress_bar,
126:             recreate_index=recreate_index,
127:             shard_number=shard_number,
128:             replication_factor=replication_factor,
129:             write_consistency_factor=write_consistency_factor,
130:             on_disk_payload=on_disk_payload,
131:             hnsw_config=hnsw_config,
132:             optimizers_config=optimizers_config,
133:             wal_config=wal_config,
134:             quantization_config=quantization_config,
135:             init_from=init_from,
136:             wait_result_from_api=wait_result_from_api,
137:             metadata=metadata,
138:             write_batch_size=write_batch_size,
139:             scroll_size=scroll_size,
140:             payload_fields_to_index=payload_fields_to_index,
141:         )
142: 
143:         self.async_client = qdrant_client.AsyncQdrantClient(
144:             location=location,
145:             url=url,
146:             port=port,
147:             grpc_port=grpc_port,
148:             prefer_grpc=prefer_grpc,
149:             https=https,
150:             api_key=api_key.resolve_value() if api_key else None,
151:             prefix=prefix,
152:             timeout=timeout,
153:             host=host,
154:             path=path,
155:             force_disable_check_same_thread=force_disable_check_same_thread,
156:             metadata=metadata or {},
157:         )
158: 
159:         # to improve the indexing performance
160:         # see https://qdrant.tech/documentation/guides/multiple-partitions/?q=mul#calibrate-performance
161:         self.client.create_payload_index(
162:             collection_name=index, field_name="project_id", field_schema="keyword"
163:         )
164: 
165:     async def _query_by_embedding(
166:         self,
167:         query_embedding: List[float],
168:         filters: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None,
169:         top_k: int = 10,
170:         scale_score: bool = True,
171:         return_embedding: bool = False,
172:     ) -> List[Document]:
173:         qdrant_filters = convert_filters_to_qdrant(filters)
174: 
175:         points = await self.async_client.search(
176:             collection_name=self.index,
177:             query_vector=rest.NamedVector(
178:                 name=DENSE_VECTORS_NAME if self.use_sparse_embeddings else "",
179:                 vector=query_embedding,
180:             ),
181:             search_params=(
182:                 rest.SearchParams(
183:                     quantization=rest.QuantizationSearchParams(
184:                         rescore=True,
185:                         oversampling=3.0,
186:                     ),
187:                 )
188:                 if len(query_embedding)
189:                 >= 1024  # reference: https://qdrant.tech/articles/binary-quantization/#when-should-you-not-use-bq
190:                 else None
191:             ),
192:             query_filter=qdrant_filters,
193:             limit=top_k,
194:             with_vectors=return_embedding,
195:         )
196:         results = [
197:             convert_qdrant_point_to_haystack_document(
198:                 point, use_sparse_embeddings=self.use_sparse_embeddings
199:             )
200:             for point in points
201:         ]
202:         if scale_score:
203:             for document in results:
204:                 score = document.score
205:                 if self.similarity == "cosine":
206:                     score = (score + 1) / 2
207:                 else:
208:                     score = float(1 / (1 + np.exp(-score / 100)))
209:                 document.score = score
210:         return results
211: 
212:     async def _query_by_filters(
213:         self,
214:         filters: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None,
215:         top_k: Optional[int] = None,
216:     ) -> List[Document]:
217:         qdrant_filters = convert_filters_to_qdrant(filters)
218:         points_list = []
219:         offset = None
220:         while True:
221:             points = await self.async_client.scroll(
222:                 collection_name=self.index,
223:                 offset=offset,
224:                 scroll_filter=qdrant_filters,
225:                 limit=top_k,
226:             )
227:             points_list.extend(points[0])
228:             if points[1] is None:
229:                 break
230:             offset = points[1]
231: 
232:         if points_list:
233:             return [
234:                 convert_qdrant_point_to_haystack_document(
235:                     point, use_sparse_embeddings=self.use_sparse_embeddings
236:                 )
237:                 for point in points_list
238:             ]
239:         else:
240:             return []
241: 
242:     async def delete_documents(self, filters: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None):
243:         if not filters:
244:             qdrant_filters = rest.Filter()
245:         else:
246:             qdrant_filters = convert_filters_to_qdrant(filters)
247: 
248:         try:
249:             await self.async_client.delete(
250:                 collection_name=self.index,
251:                 points_selector=qdrant_filters,
252:                 wait=self.wait_result_from_api,
253:             )
254:         except KeyError:
255:             logger.warning(
256:                 "Called QdrantDocumentStore.delete_documents() on a non-existing ID",
257:             )
258: 
259:     async def count_documents(self, filters: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None) -> int:
260:         if not filters:
261:             qdrant_filters = rest.Filter()
262:         else:
263:             qdrant_filters = convert_filters_to_qdrant(filters)
264: 
265:         return (
266:             await self.async_client.count(
267:                 collection_name=self.index, count_filter=qdrant_filters
268:             )
269:         ).count
270: 
271:     async def write_documents(
272:         self, documents: List[Document], policy: DuplicatePolicy = DuplicatePolicy.FAIL
273:     ):
274:         for doc in documents:
275:             if not isinstance(doc, Document):
276:                 msg = f"DocumentStore.write_documents() expects a list of Documents but got an element of {type(doc)}."
277:                 raise ValueError(msg)
278: 
279:         self._set_up_collection(
280:             self.index,
281:             self.embedding_dim,
282:             False,
283:             self.similarity,
284:             self.use_sparse_embeddings,
285:             self.sparse_idf,
286:             self.on_disk,
287:             self.payload_fields_to_index,
288:         )
289: 
290:         if len(documents) == 0:
291:             logger.warning(
292:                 "Calling QdrantDocumentStore.write_documents() with empty list"
293:             )
294:             return
295: 
296:         document_objects = self._handle_duplicate_documents(
297:             documents=documents,
298:             policy=policy,
299:         )
300: 
301:         batched_documents = document_store.get_batches_from_generator(
302:             document_objects, self.write_batch_size
303:         )
304:         with tqdm(
305:             total=len(document_objects), disable=not self.progress_bar
306:         ) as progress_bar:
307:             for document_batch in batched_documents:
308:                 batch = convert_haystack_documents_to_qdrant_points(
309:                     document_batch,
310:                     use_sparse_embeddings=self.use_sparse_embeddings,
311:                 )
312: 
313:                 await self.async_client.upsert(
314:                     collection_name=self.index,
315:                     points=batch,
316:                     wait=self.wait_result_from_api,
317:                 )
318: 
319:                 progress_bar.update(self.write_batch_size)
320:         return len(document_objects)
321: 
322: 
323: class AsyncQdrantEmbeddingRetriever(QdrantEmbeddingRetriever):
324:     def __init__(
325:         self,
326:         document_store: AsyncQdrantDocumentStore,
327:         filters: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None,
328:         top_k: int = 10,
329:         scale_score: bool = True,
330:         return_embedding: bool = False,
331:     ):
332:         super(AsyncQdrantEmbeddingRetriever, self).__init__(
333:             document_store=document_store,
334:             filters=filters,
335:             top_k=top_k,
336:             scale_score=scale_score,
337:             return_embedding=return_embedding,
338:         )
339:         self._document_store = document_store
340: 
341:     @component.output_types(documents=List[Document])
342:     async def run(
343:         self,
344:         query_embedding: List[float],
345:         filters: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None,
346:         top_k: Optional[int] = None,
347:         scale_score: Optional[bool] = None,
348:         return_embedding: Optional[bool] = None,
349:     ):
350:         if query_embedding:
351:             docs = await self._document_store._query_by_embedding(
352:                 query_embedding=query_embedding,
353:                 filters=filters or self._filters,
354:                 top_k=top_k or self._top_k,
355:                 scale_score=scale_score or self._scale_score,
356:                 return_embedding=return_embedding or self._return_embedding,
357:             )
358:         else:
359:             docs = await self._document_store._query_by_filters(
360:                 filters=filters,
361:                 top_k=top_k,
362:             )
363: 
364:         return {"documents": docs}
365: 
366: 
367: @provider("qdrant")
368: class QdrantProvider(DocumentStoreProvider):
369:     def __init__(
370:         self,
371:         location: str = os.getenv("QDRANT_HOST", "qdrant"),
372:         api_key: Optional[str] = os.getenv("QDRANT_API_KEY", None),
373:         timeout: Optional[int] = (
374:             int(os.getenv("QDRANT_TIMEOUT")) if os.getenv("QDRANT_TIMEOUT") else 120
375:         ),
376:         embedding_model_dim: int = (
377:             int(os.getenv("EMBEDDING_MODEL_DIMENSION"))
378:             if os.getenv("EMBEDDING_MODEL_DIMENSION")
379:             else 0
380:         ),
381:         recreate_index: bool = (
382:             bool(os.getenv("SHOULD_FORCE_DEPLOY"))
383:             if os.getenv("SHOULD_FORCE_DEPLOY")
384:             else False
385:         ),
386:         **_,
387:     ):
388:         self._location = location
389:         self._api_key = Secret.from_token(api_key) if api_key else None
390:         self._timeout = timeout
391:         self._embedding_model_dim = embedding_model_dim
392:         self._reset_document_store(recreate_index)
393: 
394:     def _reset_document_store(self, recreate_index: bool):
395:         self.get_store(recreate_index=recreate_index)
396:         self.get_store(dataset_name="table_descriptions", recreate_index=recreate_index)
397:         self.get_store(dataset_name="view_questions", recreate_index=recreate_index)
398:         self.get_store(dataset_name="sql_pairs", recreate_index=recreate_index)
399:         self.get_store(dataset_name="instructions", recreate_index=recreate_index)
400:         self.get_store(dataset_name="project_meta", recreate_index=recreate_index)
401: 
402:     def get_store(
403:         self,
404:         dataset_name: Optional[str] = None,
405:         recreate_index: bool = False,
406:     ):
407:         return AsyncQdrantDocumentStore(
408:             location=self._location,
409:             api_key=self._api_key,
410:             embedding_dim=self._embedding_model_dim,
411:             index=dataset_name or "Document",
412:             recreate_index=recreate_index,
413:             on_disk=True,
414:             timeout=self._timeout,
415:             quantization_config=(
416:                 rest.BinaryQuantization(
417:                     binary=rest.BinaryQuantizationConfig(
418:                         always_ram=True,
419:                     )
420:                 )
421:                 if self._embedding_model_dim >= 1024
422:                 else None
423:             ),
424:             # to improve the indexing performance, we disable building global index for the whole collection
425:             # see https://qdrant.tech/documentation/guides/multiple-partitions/?q=mul#calibrate-performance
426:             hnsw_config=rest.HnswConfigDiff(
427:                 payload_m=16,
428:                 m=0,
429:             ),
430:         )
431: 
432:     def get_retriever(
433:         self,
434:         document_store: AsyncQdrantDocumentStore,
435:         top_k: int = 10,
436:     ):
437:         return AsyncQdrantEmbeddingRetriever(
438:             document_store=document_store,
439:             top_k=top_k,
440:         )

(End of file - total 440 lines)
</content>
```

<content>
1: # Architecture
2: 
3: Wren Engine CLI is a modular Python application that transforms semantic SQL through an MDL layer before executing it against your database. This page explains how the components fit together.
4: 
5: ## Overview
6: 
7: ```text
8: ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
9: │                      Wren CLI (Typer)                    │
10: │                                                          │
11: │  --sql / query   dry-plan   dry-run   version            │
12: │  context         profile    memory    utils              │
13: └──┬──────────────┬──────────────┬──────────────┬──────────┘
14:    │              │              │              │
15:    ▼              ▼              │              ▼
16: ┌────────────┐ ┌────────────┐    │   ┌────────────────────┐
17: │ Profile    │ │ Context    │    │   │ Memory Layer       │
18: │ Mgmt       │ │ Mgmt       │    │   │ (LanceDB)          │
19: │            │ │            │    │   │                    │
20: │ ~/.wren/   │ │ init       │    │   │ schema_items       │
21: │ profiles   │ │ validate   │    │   │ query_history      │
22: │ .yml       │ │ build      │    │   │                    │
23: └─────┬──────┘ └─────┬──────┘    │   │ fetch / recall     │
24:       │              │           │   │ store / index      │
25:       │   connection │ mdl.json  │   └────────────────────┘
26:       │       info   │           │
27:       └──────┐ ┌─────┘           │
28:              ▼ ▼                 │
29:       ┌──────────────┐           │
30:       │  WrenEngine  │◄──────────┘  (dry-plan, query, dry-run)
31:       │              │
32:       │  plan()      │
33:       │  execute()   │
34:       └──┬───────┬───┘
35:          │       │
36:     plan │       │ execute
37:          │       │
38:          ▼       ▼
39: ┌──────────────┐ ┌──────────────────┐
40: │ SQL Planning │ │ Connectors       │
41: │              │ │                  │
42: │ sqlglot      │ │                  │
43: │  parse       │ │ Postgres  DuckDB │
44: │  qualify     │ │ BigQuery  MySQL  │
45: │  transpile   │ │ Snowflake Trino  │
46: │              │ │ ...18+ sources   │
47: │ CTE Rewriter │ │                  │
48: │  inject CTEs │ └──────────────────┘
49: │              │
50: │ Policy check │
51: └──────┬───────┘
52:        │
53:        ▼
54: ┌──────────────────┐
55: │ wren-core-py     │
56: │ (Rust / PyO3)    │
57: │                  │
58: │ SessionContext   │
59: │ ManifestExtractor│
60: │ transform_sql()  │
61: └──────────────────┘
62: ```
63: 
64: ## Components
65: 
66: ### CLI layer
67: 
68: The top-level command router, built on [Typer](https://typer.tiangolo.com/). It parses flags, discovers the MDL project and active profile, then delegates to WrenEngine or the appropriate subsystem.
69: 
70: | Command | What it does |
71: |---------|-------------|
72: | `wren --sql` / `wren query` | Plan + execute SQL, return results |
73: | `wren dry-plan` | Plan only — show the expanded SQL without executing |
74: | `wren dry-run` | Validate SQL against the live database without returning rows |
75: | `wren context` | Project management — init, validate, build, show |
76: | `wren profile` | Connection management — add, switch, list, debug, rm |
77: | `wren memory` | Schema indexing and NL-SQL recall |
78: | `wren utils` | Type normalization utilities |
79: 
80: ### WrenEngine
81: 
82: The central orchestrator (`engine.py`). It owns the plan-then-execute pipeline:
83: 
84: 1. Receive user SQL
85: 2. Call the SQL planning subsystem to expand MDL references
86: 3. Pass the planned SQL to a connector for execution
87: 4. Return results as a PyArrow table
88: 
89: ### SQL planning
90: 
91: Transforms user SQL from semantic model references to executable database SQL. Three libraries collaborate:
92: 
93: - **sqlglot** — parses SQL, qualifies table/column references, transpiles between dialects
94: - **CTE Rewriter** — identifies which MDL models are referenced, builds a CTE for each, and injects them into the query
95: - **wren-core-py** — Rust engine (via PyO3 bindings) that expands model definitions, resolves calculated fields, and handles relationship joins
96: 
97: The planning pipeline:
98: 
99: ```
100: User SQL (e.g. SELECT * FROM orders WHERE status = 'pending')
101:   │
102:   ├── sqlglot: parse → qualify tables → normalize identifiers
103:   ├── Extract referenced table names → ["orders"]
104:   ├── ManifestExtractor: filter MDL to only referenced models
105:   ├── Policy check (strict mode, denied functions)
106:   ├── CTE Rewriter:
107:   │     ├── For each model: wren-core transform_sql() → expanded CTE
108:   │     └── Inject CTEs into original query
109:   └── sqlglot: transpile to target dialect (postgres, bigquery, etc.)
110:         │
111:         ▼
112:   WITH "orders" AS (
113:     SELECT o_orderkey, o_custkey, o_totalprice
114:     FROM "public"."orders"
115:   )
116:   SELECT * FROM "orders" WHERE status = 'pending'
117: ```
118: 
119: ### Connectors
120: 
121: Data source connectors execute the planned SQL against the actual database. Each connector implements a common interface for query execution, dry-run validation, and connection lifecycle.
122: 
123: Supported data sources: PostgreSQL, MySQL, BigQuery, Snowflake, DuckDB, ClickHouse, Trino, SQL Server, Databricks, Redshift, Oracle, Athena, Apache Spark, and more.
124: 
125: Each connector:
126: - Receives dialect-specific SQL from the planning stage
127: - Executes against the target database
128: - Handles type coercion (Decimal, UUID, etc.)
129: - Returns a PyArrow table
130: 
131: ### Profile management
132: 
133: Stores named database connections in `~/.wren/profiles.yml`. One profile is active at a time. All `wren` commands use the active profile unless overridden with explicit flags.
134: 
135: See [Profiles](../guide/profiles.md) for details.
136: 
137: ### Context management
138: 
139: Manages the MDL project lifecycle — YAML authoring, validation, and compilation to `target/mdl.json`.
140: 
141: Key operations:
142: - `wren context init` — scaffold a new project (or import from existing `mdl.json`)
143: - `wren context validate` — check YAML structure without a database
144: - `wren context build` — compile snake_case YAML to camelCase JSON
145: - `wren context show` — display the current project summary
146: 
147: See [Wren Project](../guide/modeling/wren_project.md) for the project format.
148: 
149: ### Memory layer
150: 
151: A LanceDB-backed semantic index with two collections:
152: 
153: | Collection | Contents | Purpose |
154: |------------|----------|---------|
155: | **schema_items** | Models, columns, relationships, views | Semantic schema search per question |
156: | **query_history** | Confirmed NL → SQL pairs | Few-shot recall for similar questions |
157: 
158: The memory layer enables the self-learning loop: each confirmed query improves future recall accuracy.
159: 
160: See [Memory](../guide/memory.md) for details.
161: 
162: ### wren-core (Rust engine)
163: 
164: The core semantic engine, written in Rust and exposed to Python via PyO3 bindings (`wren-core-py`). It handles:
165: 
166: - **SessionContext** — maintains the MDL state and provides `transform_sql()` for expanding model definitions into SQL
167: - **ManifestExtractor** — filters the full MDL manifest to only the models referenced in a query, reducing planning overhead
168: - **Model expansion** — resolves `table_reference` and `ref_sql` models into physical SQL, handles calculated fields, and expands relationship joins
169: 
170: The Rust engine is where the MDL semantics are enforced — it is the source of truth for how models map to SQL.
171: 
172: ## Data flows
173: 
174: ### Query execution
175: 
176: ```
177: wren --sql "SELECT customer_id, SUM(total) FROM orders GROUP BY 1"
178:   │
179:   ├── 1. Discover MDL: project auto-discovery → target/mdl.json
180:   ├── 2. Resolve connection: active profile → ~/.wren/profiles.yml
181:   ├── 3. Plan: sqlglot parse → extract models → wren-core CTE expand → transpile
182:   ├── 4. Execute: connector → database → PyArrow table
183:   └── 5. Output: format as table / csv / json
184: ```
185: 
186: ### Project build
187: 
188: ```
189: wren context build
190:   │
191:   ├── Read wren_project.yml + models/*/ + views/*/ + relationships.yml
192:   ├── Validate structure and references
193:   ├── Convert snake_case → camelCase
194:   └── Write target/mdl.json
195: ```
196: 
197: ### Memory lifecycle
198: 
199: ```
200: wren memory index          → Parse MDL, embed schema items, store in LanceDB
201: wren memory fetch -q "..." → Embed query, search schema_items, return context
202: wren memory recall -q "..."→ Embed query, search query_history, return examples
203: wren memory store          → Embed NL-SQL pair, append to query_history
204: ```
205: 
206: ## Key dependencies
207: 
208: | Dependency | Role |
209: |------------|------|
210: | **wren-core-py** | Rust semantic engine (PyO3 bindings) |
211: | **sqlglot** | SQL parsing, qualification, dialect transpilation |
212: | **database connectors** | Data source execution layer |
213: | **pyarrow** | Query result representation |
214: | **lancedb** | Vector storage for memory layer |
215: | **sentence-transformers** | Local embeddings for memory search |
216: | **typer** | CLI framework |
217: | **pydantic** | Config and connection validation |

(End of file - total 217 lines)
</content>
```
